### 2310.09804 / midloop / alg-001

**检查内容：** In Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 (Algorithm 1), the server update is x^{t+1} = x^t - γ g^t.

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 0.870%）。

**论文定位：** Algorithm 1, line 3。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2310_09804/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:8)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:35)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_repro/algorithms.py, the `_run_vr` function implements Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 (called with `global_update=False`). The server update step is at line `x = x - gamma * g` inside the iteration loop, which directly corresponds to x^{t+1} = x^t - γ g^t. The method is dispatched via `run_algorithm` when `method_key in {"byz-vr-marina-2.0", "byz-vr-marina2", "vr-marina-2", "vr2"}`.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> Both Byz-VR-MARINA and Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 share the same `_run_vr` function; they differ only in the `global_update` flag which affects how worker gradient estimators are updated, not the server update step itself.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:35)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py, line 375 inside the main iteration loop (line 374: `for iteration in range(1, config.steps + 1):`), the server update is: `x = x - gamma * aggregate_gradient`. This directly implements x^{t+1} = x^t - γ g^t, where `gamma` is γ and `aggregate_gradient` is g^t. This update applies to all methods including "vr-marina-2" (Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0), which is listed in the method check on line 377.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The `aggregate_gradient` variable is computed as the robust aggregation (e.g., coordinate-wise median) of client gradient estimates, corresponding to g^t in Algorithm 1.

### 2310.09804 / midloop / alg-002

**检查内容：** In Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0, when coin\_t=1, each worker i computes g\_i^{t+1} = ∇f\_i(x^{t+1}) and sends it uncompressed to the server.

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 0.870%）。

**论文定位：** Algorithm 1, lines 6-7。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2310_09804/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:17)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`medium`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:46)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_repro/algorithms.py, the `_run_vr` function (called with `global_update=False` for "Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0") implements: when `sync=True` (coin\_t=1), line `next_local[i] = obj.grad(x, rows=r)` computes the full gradient ∇f\_i(x^{t+1}) for each honest worker i. This full-gradient vector (not compressed) is then propagated via `good = local.copy()` and passed to aggregation — effectively sending it uncompressed to the server. The sync condition mirrors coin\_t=1 in Algorithm 1, lines 6-7.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The bit-accounting uses `message_bits = 64 * k` unconditionally (even for the sync/uncompressed case), which is inconsistent with truly uncompressed communication. However, the actual gradient values sent are full-dimensional when sync=True, matching the algorithmic claim. The discrepancy is in cost tracking, not in the gradient computation/sending behavior itself.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:46)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py, lines ~403-412, the `vr-marina-2` branch handles the sync case (coin\_t=1). When `sync=True` (controlled by `rng.random() < p` where p is the coin flip probability), each good worker computes `estimate = logistic_grad(x, client.features, client.labels, config.l2, config.setting)` — the full gradient ∇f\_i(x^{t+1}) — and charges `bits += dimension` (not `k`), meaning the full uncompressed vector is sent. The non-sync branch (coin\_t=0) by contrast uses `randk(difference, k, rng)` and charges `bits += k`.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The coin flip variable is named `sync` in code rather than `coin_t`, but the semantics match exactly: True=full uncompressed gradient, False=compressed difference.

### 2310.09804 / midloop / alg-003

**检查内容：** In Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0, when coin\_t=0, worker i computes the stochastic gradient difference m\_i^{t+1} = Δ̂\_i(x^{t+1}, x^t).

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 0.870%）。

**论文定位：** Algorithm 1, line 9。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2310_09804/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:26)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`medium`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:57)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_repro/algorithms.py, the \_run\_vr function handles Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 (global\_update=False). When sync=False (coin\_t=0), the code computes: `delta = _gradient(obj, x, chosen, ...) - _gradient(obj, x_old, chosen, ...)` which is Δ̂\_i(x^{t+1}, x^t). However, it then applies compression: `compressed, _ = randk(delta, k, rng)` and sets `next_local[i] = local[i] + compressed`. So the stored message/local estimate is NOT `m_i^{t+1} = Δ̂_i(x^{t+1}, x^t)` as the rubric states; instead it is `m_i^{t-1} + C(Δ̂_i(x^{t+1}, x^t))`. The stochastic gradient difference is computed as an intermediate value but is immediately compressed and added to the previous local estimate before being used as the worker's message.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The distinction depends on what "computes m\_i^{t+1}" means in the paper. If the paper's Algorithm 1 line 9 refers only to computing the raw gradient difference (with compression handled separately in another line), the claim may hold. But as written, the code's final `next_local[i]` = `local[i] + randk(delta)`, not `delta` itself — making it inconsistent with m\_i^{t+1} = Δ̂\_i(x^{t+1}, x^t).

**B：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:57)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py lines 377-394, the `vr-marina-2` branch handles the coin\_t=0 case (when `sync` is False). It computes: `difference = _batch_gradient(x, ...) - _batch_gradient(previous_x, ...)` (the stochastic gradient difference), but then sets `base = local[worker]` (the worker's previous estimate) and returns `estimate = base + randk(difference, k, rng)`. This is NOT simply `m_i^{t+1} = Δ̂_i(x^{t+1}, x^t)`; instead it computes `m_i^{t+1} = m_i^t + C(Δ̂_i(x^{t+1}, x^t))` — adding the compressed difference to the previous estimate. The plain gradient difference alone is never stored as the estimate when coin\_t=0.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The VR-MARINA (not 2.0) variant uses `base = aggregate_gradient` (line 390), while VR-MARINA 2.0 uses `base = local[worker]`. Neither case implements the pure `m_i^{t+1} = Δ̂_i(x^{t+1}, x^t)` formula claimed in the rubric for coin\_t=0.

### 2310.09804 / midloop / alg-004

**检查内容：** In Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0, when coin\_t=0, worker i compresses m\_i^{t+1} as c\_i^t = Q\_i(m\_i^{t+1}).

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 0.870%）。

**论文定位：** Algorithm 1, line 10。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2310_09804/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:35)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:68)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In algorithms.py, Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 is implemented via \_run\_vr(..., global\_update=False). When sync=False (coin\_t=0), lines 133-136 compute: delta = grad(x, batch) - grad(x\_old, batch); compressed, \_ = randk(delta, k, rng); next\_local\[i\] = local\[i\] + compressed. The compressor Q (randk) is applied to 'delta' (the stochastic gradient difference), NOT to m\_i^{t+1} (the full variance-reduced gradient estimator). The paper's Algorithm 1 line 10 specifies c\_i^t = Q\_i(m\_i^{t+1}), meaning compression is applied to the full estimator m\_i^{t+1}. The code instead uses an error-feedback style update where only the incremental difference is compressed, then added to the running local estimate.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code compresses 'delta' (grad difference) rather than the full m\_i^{t+1}. These are mathematically distinct: Q(local\[i\] + delta) != local\[i\] + Q(delta) in general. The implementation does not match the specific claim in Algorithm 1 line 10.

**B：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:68)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py, the vr-marina-2 branch (lines ~290-305) handles the coin\_t=0 case (sync=False). The code computes: `base = local[worker]` (i.e., m\_i^t), then `estimate = base + randk(difference, k, rng)` = m\_i^t + Q(∇f\_i(x^{t+1},ξ) - ∇f\_i(x^t,ξ)). This applies compression Q (via randk) to the \*gradient difference\*, not to m\_i^{t+1}. The paper's Algorithm 1 line 10 specifies c\_i^t = Q\_i(m\_i^{t+1}), meaning: first compute the full new momentum m\_i^{t+1}, then compress it. The code never constructs m\_i^{t+1} as a standalone vector before compressing — it computes m\_i^t + compressed\_diff instead, which is a different (EF21-style) update scheme, not the direct Q\_i(m\_i^{t+1}) compression described in the paper.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code's approach (m\_i^t + Q(diff)) is mathematically related but structurally different from Q\_i(m\_i^{t+1}). The paper's formulation compresses the full new momentum vector, while the code compresses only the incremental difference and adds it to the old estimate. These are not equivalent.

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:79)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py, the `vr-marina-2` branch (lines ~310-330) handles the coin\_t=0 case (sync=False). When method=="vr-marina-2" and sync is False: `base = local[worker]` (i.e., g\_i^t), then `estimate = base + randk(difference, k, rng)` (i.e., g\_i^t + c\_i^t, where c\_i^t is the RandK-compressed stochastic difference). This exactly matches g\_i^{t+1} = g\_i^t + c\_i^t. The differentiation from vr-marina (which uses aggregate\_gradient as base) confirms this is specific to vr-marina-2.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The variable `sync` corresponds to coin\_t=1 (full gradient sync), so `not sync` (sync=False) corresponds to coin\_t=0. The randk compressed difference serves as c\_i^t.

### 2310.09804 / midloop / alg-006

**检查内容：** In Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0, the server aggregates the received gradient estimates using an agnostic robust aggregator: g^{t+1} = ARAgg(g\_1^{t+1}, ..., g\_n^{t+1}).

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 0.870%）。

**论文定位：** Algorithm 1, line 15。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2310_09804/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:53)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:90)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_repro/algorithms.py, the function \_run\_vr (line 102) is invoked for "Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0" (line 259). At line 141, after stacking good and Byzantine worker gradient estimates into `vectors = np.vstack([good, bad])`, the server computes `g = aggregate(vectors, aggregation, 2, rng)`. The `aggregate` function in src/byz\_repro/aggregation.py (line 44–53) dispatches to coordinate\_median, geometric\_median, or mean depending on the `aggregation` parameter — all serving as the agnostic robust aggregator (ARAgg) from the paper. This directly corresponds to Algorithm 1, line 15: g^{t+1} = ARAgg(g\_1^{t+1}, ..., g\_n^{t+1}).

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> Both Byz-VR-MARINA and Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 share the same \_run\_vr function; the `global_update` flag distinguishes them. The aggregation step at line 141 is identical for both variants.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:90)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py, the `run()` function handles the `vr-marina-2` method. At line 445, after workers compute gradient estimates and Byzantine workers inject attacks (lines 431-443), the server aggregates all gradient estimates using `aggregate_gradient = aggregate(local, config.aggregator, rng, config.bucket_size)`. The `aggregate()` function (line 224) supports robust aggregators including coordinate-wise median with bucketing ("CM", implemented as `coordinate_median_bucket`) and geometric median ("GM"), both of which are agnostic robust aggregators. This `aggregate_gradient` is then used in the server update step `x = x - gamma * aggregate_gradient` (line 375 in the next iteration). For `vr-marina-2` specifically, the base estimate per worker is `local[worker]` (line 390, not the aggregate), consistent with the Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 formulation where each worker sends its own estimate and the server aggregates them via ARAgg.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code uses "CM" (coordinate-wise median) or "GM" (geometric median) as the aggregator, which qualifies as an agnostic robust aggregator. The aggregation applies to all n workers (good + Byzantine), which matches Algorithm 1 line 15.

### 2310.09804 / midloop / alg-007

**检查内容：** In Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0, the local gradient estimates are initialized as g\_i^0 = ∇f\_i(x^0) for all i ∈ G.

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`secondary`；权重 1（本层占比 0.435%）。

**论文定位：** Theorem 2.1。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2310_09804/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:62)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：通过，1/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:101)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_repro/algorithms.py, the `_run_vr` function (called for Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 via `global_update=False`) initializes `local = np.vstack([obj.grad(x, rows=r) for r in rows[: workers - byzantine]])` where `x = x0` at that point. This computes the full gradient of each good worker's local objective at x^0, giving g\_i^0 = ∇f\_i(x^0) for all i in G (the `workers - byzantine` good workers). The `global_update=False` path uses `local[i]` (not `g`) for updates, confirming this is the VR-MARINA 2.0 variant.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The `rows` parameter contains one row-index array per worker; `obj.grad(x, rows=r)` computes the full gradient over worker i's local data, matching ∇f\_i(x^0).

This does not match the paper's Algorithm 2 line 13 form `g_i^{t+1} = g_i^t + a * c_i^t`, because: (1) there is no explicit `a` scaling factor applied to the compressed term, (2) the correction term incorporates a `momentum` factor which is not part of the stated formula, and (3) the variable `coin_t` does not exist in code; sync probability is used instead. The code implements a momentum-based correction update rather than the simple `g_i^t + a * c_i^t` form.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The `a` parameter in the paper may equal 1 in certain settings (making `a * c_i^t` = `c_i^t`), but the momentum term in the correction formula is an additional structural difference. The code appears to implement a variant of DASHA-PAGE with compression, but the exact line 13 update formula with `a * c_i^t` is not directly reproduced.

For EF21-BC, `downlink_state = x.copy()` initializes w^0 = x^0 (line ~315). Both conditions in the claim are directly implemented for the `ef21` and `ef21-bc` methods.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The initialization covers ALL clients (including Byzantine), though for Byzantine workers only good workers (range(good)) matter per the algorithm. The good workers' estimates are correctly initialized as ∇f\_i(x^0).

### 2310.09804 / midloop / alg-031

**检查内容：** In Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 and Byz-DASHA-PAGE, a single global coin flip with probability p determines whether all workers compute full gradients or stochastic gradient differences at each iteration.

**类别与权重：** `algorithm_impl`；`secondary`；权重 1（本层占比 0.435%）。

**论文定位：** Algorithm 1 line 5, Algorithm 2 line 5。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2310_09804/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:278)

**如何检查：** algorithm_impl 无单独 verification spec，Judge 对照描述、论文上下文及仓库代码作二元判断。

**A：通过，1/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:365)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_repro/algorithms.py, both \_run\_vr (used for Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 via global\_update=False) and \_run\_dasha (Byz-DASHA-PAGE) perform a single global coin flip per iteration: `sync = bool(rng.random() < probability)` (line ~107 in \_run\_vr, line ~143 in \_run\_dasha). All workers then branch on this shared `sync` variable: if True, each worker computes a full gradient (obj.grad(x, rows=r) in \_run\_vr; full gradient reset for h\[i\] in \_run\_dasha); if False, each computes a stochastic gradient difference (delta = gradient(x) - gradient(x\_old) with a random mini-batch). The single draw of `sync` is shared across all workers in both algorithms.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> In Byz-DASHA-PAGE, the sync event resets the local variance-reducer h\[i\] to a full gradient but still always communicates a compressed difference; the code comment acknowledges this. The core claim — a single global Bernoulli(p) flip determines full-gradient vs. stochastic-difference computation by all workers — holds for both algorithms.

**B：通过，1/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:365)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py, within the `run` function: (1) `p = min(1.0, config.batch_fraction) if config.method.lower() in {"vr-marina", "vr-marina-2", "dasha-page"} else 1.0` sets the coin-flip probability. (2) Inside the iteration loop: `sync = method in {"vr-marina", "vr-marina-2", "dasha-page"} and rng.random() < p` performs a single global coin flip. (3) For both "vr-marina-2" (Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0) and "dasha-page" (Byz-DASHA-PAGE), the same `sync` boolean is used for all workers: if `sync` is True all workers compute full gradient (`logistic_grad` over full client data); if `sync` is False all workers compute stochastic gradient differences via mini-batch sampling and `randk` compression.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The variable name is `sync` in the code (rather than a coin-flip variable named `p`), but the mechanism is exactly a single Bernoulli(p) draw per iteration applied globally to all workers for both algorithms.

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In configs/formal.json, probability=0.01 (batch\_fraction), and momentum=0.05263157894736842 (=1/19=1/(2\*10-1) where randk\_fraction=0.1 gives k=d\*0.1, so d/k=10). In run\_experiment.py lines 100-103, momentum for DASHA is computed inline as 1.0/(2.0\*(d/k)-1.0)=1/19, matching the theoretical formula; for other methods it reads the config value (also 1/19). However, probability is set as min(batch\_fraction, 1/(d/k)) = min(0.01, 0.1) = 0.01 for VR-MARINA methods, and batch\_fraction=0.01 for DASHA — NOT the theoretically optimal p=1/(d/k)=0.1. README.md line 43 confirms: 'b/m=0.01 Bernoulli probability' is used, not the theory-optimal value. Only momentum matches the theoretical optimum; probability does not.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> Momentum a = 1/(2\*omega-1) where omega=d/k matches the DASHA-PAGE theoretical optimum. But probability p is set to batch\_fraction=0.01 instead of the theoretically optimal 1/omega=0.1, so the joint claim (both a and p are theoretically optimal) fails.

The momentum parameter at line 370 is `momentum = 1.0 / max(1.0, 2.0 * (dimension / k) - 1.0)` which matches the theoretical form 1/(2ω-1), but the docs/formal\_experiment\_report.md explicitly states: "The official repository is notebook-oriented and does not publish every per-dataset finetuned stepsize or attack-strength constant. This repository therefore uses one conservative deterministic stepsize proxy." The stepsize (`_theoretical_gamma`) is also a "conservative scale" heuristic (base=0.12 or 0.08) rather than the exact theoretical expression from the paper.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The momentum formula 1/(2ω-1) for DASHA-PAGE matches theory, but p is tied to batch\_fraction (0.01) rather than the theoretically-optimal compression-fraction-based value, and the step size is a conservative proxy rather than the paper's exact theoretical expression. The deviation report in docs confirms this.

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:519)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_repro/algorithms.py lines 58-65, \_attack\_vectors() implements all four attacks: BF (bit flipping: message = -full\_grad), LF (label flipping: message = obj.grad\_with\_labels(x, -obj.y)), IPM (inner product manipulation: message = -z \* np.mean(good, axis=0)), and ALIE (a little is enough: message = np.mean(good, axis=0) - 1.06 \* np.std(good, axis=0)). configs/formal.json lists attacks as \["BF", "LF", "IPM", "ALIE"\]. The resolved config for the homogeneous phishing experiment (results/formal/exp\_006\_phishing\_homogeneous/config\_resolved.json) also shows \['BF', 'LF', 'IPM', 'ALIE'\].

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

>

> 2\. `src/byz_compression/core.py` lines 254-261: `attack_vectors()` function implements all four:
>    \- BF (Bit Flipping): `return -full_gradient` (line 254-255)
>    \- LF (Label Flipping): `return logistic_grad(x, malicious_data.features, -malicious_data.labels, l2, setting)` (lines 256-257)
>    \- IPM (Inner Product Manipulation): `return -float(strength) * mean_good` (lines 258-259)
>    \- ALIE (A Little Is Enough): `return mean_good - 1.06 * np.std(good_vectors, axis=0)` (lines 260-261)
> 3\. `run_experiment.py` line 53 confirms the default attacks list: `"attacks": ["BF", "LF", "IPM", "ALIE"]`.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The formal config explicitly targets the phishing dataset with all four attacks. Implementation is in core.py's attack\_vectors() function.

**A：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`medium`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:530)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> The repository implements a `homogeneous` flag in `src/byz_repro/data.py` (line 30-35): when `homogeneous=True`, every good worker receives the full dataset (`rows = [np.arange(A.shape[0]) for _ in range(good)]`). The experiment `exp_006_phishing_homogeneous` (config at `results/formal/exp_006_phishing_homogeneous/config_resolved.json`) uses `"homogeneous": true` with the phishing dataset. `SOURCES.md` documents "homogeneous clients each receive the full data." However, no file in the repository explicitly maps the homogeneous setting to B=0 and ζ=0, nor references Assumption 1.4 by name or by these parameter symbols. Searched all .py, .md, and .json files for "B.\*=.\*0", "zeta.\*0", "ζ.\*0", "Assumption 1.4" — no matches found.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The mathematical equivalence holds (giving all workers identical data trivially makes B=0 and ζ=0), but the repository never explicitly states this correspondence using the paper's notation from Assumption 1.4. The rubric is checking whether this specific correspondence is declared/documented in the code, which it is not.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`medium`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:530)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py lines 80-95, the `split_clients` function with `homogeneous=True` gives every client (including Byzantine ones) the full dataset: `clients = [ClientData(dataset.features, dataset.labels, i >= good) for i in range(num_workers)]`. This means all good workers compute gradients on identical data, making local gradient variance zero (B=0, ζ=0 in Assumption 1.4). The formal config configs/formal.json line 4 includes `"homogeneous": [false, true]`, and run\_experiment.py runs both settings. The experiment\_id `exp1_homogeneous_nonconvex_phishing` is covered by the formal run with homogeneous=true, dataset=phishing, setting=NC. The code implements homogeneous correctly as full-data sharing, which operationally satisfies B=0, ζ=0. However, neither the code nor docs explicitly label this relationship as 'Assumption 1.4' or 'B=0, ζ=0' — the connection is implicit.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code correctly implements the homogeneous setting as all clients sharing the full dataset (zero gradient diversity), which is the mathematical content of B=0 and ζ=0. No explicit annotation ties this to Assumption 1.4 by name, but the implementation matches the theoretical definition.

These are passed directly to run\_algorithm() in scripts/run\_experiment.py (line ~90: gamma=float(config\["stepsizes"\]\[method\])). **证据（Judge 原文）：**

The `_theoretical_gamma` function (core.py lines 302-311) returns a single fixed conservative stepsize; there is no loop, grid, or sweep over {2^k × γ\_th} multipliers. No code in run\_experiment.py, core.py, or scripts/ performs a search over stepsize candidates before selecting a best gamma. Keywords searched: `2**`, `pow.*2`, `tune`, `grid`, `sweep`, `best_gamma`, `gamma_th`, `multiplier`, `scale.*gamma` — none matched any tuning logic.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The paper's Appendix I.1 describes fine-tuning the stepsize within {2^k × γ\_th}, but the reproduction code uses a single fixed theoretical stepsize with no such grid search.

Stochastic gradient sampling uses `rng.choice(client.features.shape[0], size=size, replace=False)` — explicitly without replacement — in the VR-MARINA, VR-MARINA-2, and DASHA-PAGE branches (lines ~270-300 in core.py).

> In src/byz\_repro/data.py, function split\_workers() (lines 30-40): when homogeneous=False (heterogeneous setting), good workers (workers - byzantine = 16 - 3 = 13) receive non-overlapping, evenly-divided contiguous slices: `rows = [np.arange(i * usable // good, (i + 1) * usable // good, dtype=int) for i in range(good)]`. Byzantine workers each receive the full dataset: `rows.extend([np.arange(A.shape[0], dtype=int) for _ in range(byzantine)])`. The config configs/formal.json confirms workers=16, byzantine=3, homogeneous=false, dataset=w8a, matching the exp3\_heterogeneous\_nonconvex\_w8a experiment.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The 13 good workers get evenly-sized non-overlapping partitions (with at most `good-1` remainder samples dropped via integer division). The 3 Byzantine workers each get all samples.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:684)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> src/byz\_compression/core.py, function split\_clients (lines 80-111): when homogeneous=False (heterogeneous setting), good = num\_workers - num\_byzantine = 16-3 = 13. Each good worker receives a non-overlapping contiguous slice of size usable//good (lines 100-108). Byzantine workers each receive the full trimmed dataset (lines 109-111: `ClientData(trimmed.features, trimmed.labels, True)`). configs/formal.json confirms num\_workers=16, num\_byzantine=3, datasets includes w8a, and homogeneous=\[false,true\] so heterogeneous runs use homogeneous=false.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The "full dataset" given to Byzantine workers is the trimmed version (trimmed to usable = (samples//good)\*good), which is effectively the full dataset minus any remainder samples dropped for even division.

This k\_rand value is passed as the `k` parameter for all methods except EF21-based ones. The compression.py file (found via grep) references RandK. **证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py: (1) `randk()` function (line 167) implements RandK unbiased sparsification, scaling selected coordinates by `vector.size / k` to ensure unbiasedness. (2) k is computed as `k = max(1, min(dimension, int(round(config.compression_fraction * dimension))))` (line 351), so K = 0.1 \* d when compression\_fraction=0.1. (3) configs/formal.json sets `"compression_fraction": 0.1` and includes dataset `"w8a"` in the `"datasets"` list. (4) `randk` is called in the update rules at lines 391 and 412. The default formal config also confirms `compression_fraction: 0.1` in the `_default_config` function of run\_experiment.py.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The heterogeneous (homogeneous=false) w8a nonconvex (NC) experiment uses the same compression setting as all other experiments in the formal config.

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py line 356: `p = min(1.0, config.batch_fraction) if config.method.lower() in {"vr-marina", "vr-marina-2", "dasha-page"} else 1.0`. With batch\_fraction=0.01 (set in configs/formal.json line 12 and run\_experiment.py line 59), this evaluates to p=0.01 for all three methods (vr-marina=Byz-VR-MARINA, vr-marina-2=Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0, dasha-page=Byz-DASHA-PAGE). The batch\_fraction=0.01 is passed as RunConfig.batch\_fraction and used in the sync probability at run\_experiment.py line 213 via `batch_fraction=float(config["batch_fraction"])`. The paper formula for vr-marina uses min{1/(1+ω), b/m}, and since b/m=0.01 is less than 1/(1+ω) for reasonable compression, the result is still p=0.01.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The code implementation uses `min(1.0, batch_fraction)` rather than `min(1/(1+omega), batch_fraction)` for vr-marina variants, but with batch\_fraction=0.01 the result is still p=0.01. The paper formula for the VR-MARINA methods includes a 1/(1+ω) term that the code does not explicitly compute, but the final p value of 0.01 matches the rubric claim.

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> scripts/run\_experiment.py line 138: axes\[0\].set\_xlabel("Cumulative communicated bits"); line 136: axes\[0\].semilogy(run\["bits"\], ...) uses bits as x-axis. SOURCES.md line 10: "Communication is counted as 64 bits per transmitted floating-point coordinate, with Rand-K/Top-K payload sizes, and is applied to every worker message." src/byz\_repro/compression.py lines 3-4: "The returned bit count is an explicit accounting convention: 64 bits per transmitted floating point value." The CSV output (curves.csv) has a 'bits' column tracking cumulative communicated bits per iteration.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The metric is described as "cumulative communicated bits" (bits sent from workers to server). The rubric says "bits sent to the server" which matches this convention exactly — all bit counting is applied to worker-to-server messages.

**B：未通过，0/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:783)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> The implementation uses a variable named `bits` (core.py lines 365–426, run\_experiment.py line 148), but the metric it tracks is **communication coordinates** (number of float components sent), not actual bits. This is confirmed by: (1) The plot x-axis label in run\_experiment.py line 156: "Communicated coordinates (64-bit units)" — explicitly "coordinates", not "bits". (2) CLAIMS.json lists the metric as "communication coordinates" in all claims. (3) In core.py, `bits += k` increments by k coordinates (RandK/TopK selects k coordinate indices), and `bits += dimension` means a full gradient (dimension coordinates). The metrics.csv column is named `bits` but represents coordinate counts. The rubric's claim that the metric is "bits sent to the server" does not match; the actual metric is communicated coordinates (number of 64-bit floating point values).

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The variable is named `bits` and could be interpreted as 64-bit units (multiply coordinates by 64 to get bits), but the code and documentation consistently describe it as "coordinates", and the plot axis label explicitly says "Communicated coordinates (64-bit units)" — not "bits sent to the server". The distinction between coordinates and bits matters for the rubric claim.

**类别与权重：** `exp_setup`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 0.870%）。

**论文定位：** Appendix I.1, I.1.2。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2310_09804/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_midloop.json:918)

**A：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:794)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_repro/objectives.py, LogisticObjective implements ridge regularization for the "pl" regime: `value += self.l2 * 0.5 * float(np.dot(x, x))` (i.e., λ/2 \* \|\|x\|\|^2) and the gradient adds `self.l2 * x`. The constructor default is `l2: float = 0.1`. In configs/formal.json, the experiment config explicitly sets `"l2": 0.1`, uses `"regimes": ["nonconvex", "pl"]`, and includes both datasets: `{"dataset_id": "libsvm_phishing", "file": "phishing"}` and `{"dataset_id": "libsvm_w8a", "file": "w8a"}`.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The regularizer is expressed as λ/2 \* \|\|x\|\|^2 in the code (l2 \* 0.5 \* dot(x,x)), which is the standard ridge form where λ = 0.1. This matches the claim r(x) = \|\|x\|\|^2 with λ = 0.1.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:794)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> run\_experiment.py line 58: "l2": 0.1 (formal profile, datasets \["phishing","w8a"\]), line 75: "l2": 0.1 (smoke profile). core.py lines 125-136: logistic\_loss and logistic\_grad implement logistic regression. Lines 116-122: \_regularizer for "PL" setting returns (0.5\*\|\|x\|\|^2, x), i.e. ridge regularization r(x) = 0.5\*\|\|x\|\|^2, scaled by l2=0.1. RunConfig dataclass (line 292) defaults l2=0.1. The experiment covers datasets \["phishing","w8a"\] and settings \["NC","PL"\] (run\_experiment.py lines 49-50).

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The regularizer for PL is 0.5\*\|\|x\|\|^2 (with factor 0.5), but this is equivalent to ridge regularization up to a constant factor absorbed in λ. The effective regularization is λ\*0.5\*\|\|x\|\|^2 = 0.05\*\|\|x\|\|^2, which could be interpreted as λ=0.1 with r(x)=0.5\|\|x\|\|^2, or λ=0.05 with r(x)=\|\|x\|\|^2. The code uses l2=0.1 as the regularization parameter regardless.

**A：未通过，0/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`medium`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:904)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In scripts/run\_experiment.py lines 101-104, the probability for non-DASHA methods is set to `min(float(config["batch_fraction"]), 1.0 / (A.shape[1] / k_rand))`. With `batch_fraction=0.01` and `randk_fraction=0.1` (so `k/d=0.1`), this evaluates to `min(0.01, 0.1)=0.01`. The theoretical optimal p for MARINA-type methods is `p = k/d = 0.1`, but the code uses `0.01` instead. Momentum for DASHA is correctly set to `1/(2*(d/k)-1)` (line 102-103), and config momentum=0.05263≈1/19 matches this for randk\_fraction=0.1, so momentum is set optimally. However, p is not at the theoretical optimum for MARINA methods.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The momentum parameter appears to be set to the theoretically optimal value for all methods, but the probability p is set to batch\_fraction=0.01 rather than the theoretical optimum k/d=0.1. The rubric requires BOTH parameters to be at their theoretical optima.

**B：未通过，0/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:904)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In core.py line 356, probability p is set as `p = min(1.0, config.batch_fraction)` (= 0.01 from configs/formal.json), which is just the batch fraction and not derived from any theoretical optimality condition. The momentum parameter a is set at line 370 as `momentum = 1.0 / max(1.0, 2.0 * (dimension / k) - 1.0)`, which corresponds to `1/(2*omega+1)` where omega=d/k-1 (a compression-parameter formula), but p is not theoretically optimal. The theoretical optimal p from Appendix I.1 would be derived from the convergence bound parameters (smoothness constants, worker counts, etc.), not simply equal to the batch sampling fraction.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The momentum formula `1/(2*(d/k)-1)` may match one standard theoretical choice for the compression parameter, but the probability p is not set to the theoretically optimal value — it is hardcoded to equal batch\_fraction (0.01). The rubric requires BOTH parameters to be set to theory-predicted optimal values.

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:959)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In `src/byz_repro/data.py`, lines 36-38, the `split_workers` function handles the heterogeneous case (when `homogeneous=False`). It computes `usable = (A.shape[0] // good) * good` and then assigns `rows = [np.arange(i * usable // good, (i + 1) * usable // good, dtype=int) for i in range(good)]`. This creates equal-sized, non-overlapping consecutive index ranges for each good worker — exactly "evenly distributed without any overlap." The `run_experiment.py` (line 85) calls `split_workers(..., homogeneous=homogeneous)` where `homogeneous=False` by default (the heterogeneous case), confirming this path is taken for the heterogeneous experiments on phishing and w8a datasets.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The implementation is straightforward: in the heterogeneous branch it computes equal consecutive partitions of the data indices with no overlap between good workers.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:959)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> src/byz\_compression/core.py lines 80-113: The `split_clients` function, when `homogeneous=False` (the heterogeneous setting), trims the dataset to `usable = (dataset.samples // good) * good` samples (line 96), computes `local_size = usable // good` (line 100), then assigns each good worker a contiguous, non-overlapping slice `features[i * local_size : (i + 1) * local_size]` (lines 101-108). This guarantees equal-sized, non-overlapping partitions. Byzantine workers receive the full dataset (lines 109-112). Both `phishing` and `w8a` datasets are configured in run\_experiment.py lines 26-27, and the heterogeneous setting (`homogeneous=False`) is the default for the formal experiment profile (line 51).

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The claim is fully satisfied: good workers get equal-sized, non-overlapping data slices in the heterogeneous setting for both phishing and w8a datasets.

In core.py line 351, k is computed as max(1, min(dimension, int(round(config.compression\_fraction \* dimension)))), meaning k = 10% of feature dimension. For phishing (68 features) k≈7; for w8a (300 features) k≈30. There is no code path or config that fixes K=1 for Byz-EF21.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The rubric claims K=1 (i.e., 1-sparse Top-K). The code uses Top-K with k=10% of dimension, which is a very different setting. No alternative config for exp5\_error\_feedback was found that would set K=1.

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_repro/objectives.py, the TwoGroupQuadraticObjective class (lines ~82-130) implements the local objective as "0.5\*\|\|x\|\|^2 + \<zeta\_group, x\>" per its docstring. The loss() method returns `0.5 * float(np.dot(x, x)) + float(np.mean(self.zeta_rows, axis=0).dot(x))` and grad() returns `x + offsets.mean(axis=0)`. In scripts/run\_neighborhood.py, the G1 group is constructed with `zeta_rows = np.vstack([np.repeat(zeta1[None, :], 100, axis=0), ...])` where the first 100 rows use zeta1 (G1). Good workers in the first half (i=0..9, rows i\*10..(i+1)\*10-1, all within rows 0..99) thus have local objective f\_i(x) = 0.5\|\|x\|\|^2 + \<zeta1, x\>, matching the rubric claim exactly.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The experiment ID in the script is exp\_003\_neighborhood, which corresponds to the neighborhood size experiment. The implementation matches the paper's Appendix I.2 specification for G1 workers.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:1091)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/quadratic.py, the experiment defines zeta1 = np.ones(config.dimension) and assigns it to the first good//2 workers (G1). The local gradient for each worker is computed as `x + zetas[worker]`, which for G1 workers equals x + zeta1. This is exactly the gradient of f\_i(x) = 1/2 \|\|x\|\|^2 + ⟨ζ1, x⟩, confirming the claim. The zetas array is built as: `np.vstack([zeta1] * (good // 2) + [zeta2] * (good - good // 2) + [...])`, so the first good//2 workers (G1) get zeta1.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The function value itself is not explicitly computed (only gradients are used in the optimization), but the gradient x + zeta1 uniquely corresponds to f\_i(x) = 1/2 \|\|x\|\|^2 + ⟨ζ1, x⟩ up to a constant.

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In scripts/run\_experiment.py lines 100-103, for Byz-DASHA-PAGE: momentum is set as `1.0 / (2.0 * (A.shape[1] / k_rand) - 1.0)` — a theoretically optimal formula based on dataset dimension and k. However, probability is set as `float(config["batch_fraction"])` = 0.01 (a fixed config value from configs/formal.json), not derived from a theoretical formula. The config value `probability: 0.01` and `batch_fraction: 0.01` are manually specified, not computed from theoretical optimal conditions. Therefore only `a` (momentum) is set to its theoretical optimum, while `p` (probability) is a manually configured hyperparameter.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The theoretical optimal `p` for DASHA-PAGE (Appendix I.1) depends on problem parameters like the variance-to-smoothness ratio. The code uses a fixed value `p=0.01`. Momentum `a = 1/(2(d/k)-1)` is correctly derived from theory. Since the claim requires BOTH to be set optimally, verdict is 0. However, if the paper's Appendix I.1 defines the optimal `p` as a batch fraction that can be chosen freely (not formula-derived), verdict may be 1.

**B：通过，1/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`medium`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:1278)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py: line 370 sets momentum (parameter a) = 1.0 / max(1.0, 2.0 \* (dimension / k) - 1.0), which is the theoretical formula 1/(2ω-1) where ω=d/k is the compression ratio. Line 356 sets p = min(1.0, config.batch\_fraction), equaling batch\_fraction (=0.01 per configs/formal.json). Both formulas correspond to standard theoretical optimal choices for Byz-DASHA-PAGE. The method is referenced as 'dasha-page' throughout, applied to phishing and w8a datasets (configs/formal.json line 2), with LMP and ROP attacks supported (core.py lines 262-266, ATTACKS tuple line 482).

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The paper's Appendix I.1 exact formulas are not reproduced here for direct comparison, but the implemented formulas (a=1/(2ω-1), p=batch\_fraction) are the standard theoretical optimal values cited for DASHA-PAGE-type algorithms. There is no separate exp8 config; the same parameter formulas apply across all experiments. Confidence is medium because we cannot compare directly with the paper's Appendix I.1 closed-form expressions without reading the paper itself.

CLAIMS.json has claim C3 for "Appendix I.4 and I.5" but its methods list is \["vr-marina", "vr-marina-2", "dasha-page", "ef21"\] with required\_baselines:\["Byz-VR-MARINA"\] — not the specific trio {Byz-VR-MARINA, Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0, Byz-DASHA-PAGE}. The trio appears in claim C2 (CLAIMS.json:24) as required\_baselines but for "Figures 6-7 and 10", not Appendix I.4/Figure 9. No script or config isolates exactly these 3 methods for an "additional attacks" experiment.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> All three methods (vr-marina, vr-marina-2, dasha-page) are implemented in src/byz\_compression/core.py and are run together in the single formal experiment alongside ef21/ef21-bc, but the rubric asks specifically about exp8\_additional\_attacks with exactly these 3 methods — no such dedicated experiment exists.

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> src/byz\_repro/objectives.py: LogisticObjective class implements binary logistic regression (loss = logaddexp(0, -y\*Ax).mean()) with a non-convex regularizer (λ/2 \* sum(x²/(1+x²)), gradient: λ\*x/(1+x²)²). The regularization parameter λ defaults to 0.1 and is set to 0.1 in configs/formal.json ("l2": 0.1). The "nonconvex" regime is included in the formal.json config's "regimes" list and is used in all formal experiments via load\_libsvm(..., l2=config\["l2"\], regularizer=regime).

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> There is no experiment named "exp9\_biased\_vs\_unbiased" in the repository (experiments are exp\_001–exp\_006), but the underlying problem setup (binary logistic regression + non-convex regularizer + λ=0.1) is fully and correctly implemented.

**B：通过，2/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_midloop.json:1399)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> In src/byz\_compression/core.py: (1) Binary logistic regression is implemented via `logistic_loss` and `logistic_grad` functions (lines ~125–135) using sigmoid/logaddexp loss. (2) Non-convex regularizer is implemented in `_regularizer` for setting="NC": `0.5 * sum(x²/(1+x²))` — a standard non-convex MCP-style regularizer (lines ~116–122). (3) Regularization parameter λ=0.1 is the default in `RunConfig.l2: float = 0.1` (line ~292) and confirmed in CLAIMS.json: "lambda=0.1". (4) The default setting for RunConfig is `setting: str = "NC"` (non-convex), used in exp9 biased\_vs\_unbiased experiments.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The PL (Polyak-Łojasiewicz) setting uses a convex L2 regularizer, but the NC setting uses the non-convex regularizer. For exp9\_biased\_vs\_unbiased the NC setting is the relevant one and it is correctly implemented with λ=0.1.

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> Searched all Python files: core.py, quadratic.py, run\_experiment.py, scripts/run\_quadratic\_formal.py. The momentum parameter `a` is computed as `1.0 / max(1.0, 2.0*(dimension/k) - 1.0)` (core.py:370, quadratic.py:44), which is a formula based on the compression ratio. However, the probability `p` is simply set to `min(1.0, config.batch_fraction)` (core.py:356), not to an optimal theoretical value. Most critically, there is no mention of Karimireddy et al. (2022), Definition 1.1, or a constant `c` anywhere in the codebase. No formula for `c` from Karimireddy et al. (2022) is implemented. The experiment id `exp9_biased_vs_unbiased` does not appear anywhere in the repository.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The momentum formula present in the code may correspond to a theoretical optimum, but the probability `p` is simply the user-configured batch\_fraction, and the constant `c` from Definition 1.1 via Karimireddy et al. (2022) is entirely absent from the codebase.

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> File: results/formal/exp\_006\_phishing\_homogeneous/metrics.json and curves.csv (regime=nonconvex, homogeneous=True, dataset=libsvm\_phishing). At the full communication budget (245,760 bits), the best (minimum) grad\_norm\_sq achieved across 4 seeds: Byz-DASHA-PAGE=6.158e-03, Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0=6.010e-03, Byz-VR-MARINA=7.639e-03. Constraint 'Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 \> Byz-VR-MARINA' holds (6.010e-03 \< 7.639e-03). Constraint 'Byz-DASHA-PAGE \> Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0' FAILS: Byz-DASHA-PAGE's best grad\_norm\_sq (6.158e-03) is higher (worse) than Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 (6.010e-03). Moreover, Byz-DASHA-PAGE is highly unstable — the 4-seed average final grad\_norm\_sq is ~5.78e-02 (one seed reached 1.98e-01), far worse than Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0's stable ~6.04e-03.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The experiment output is from exp\_006\_phishing\_homogeneous which directly matches the claim's setting (phishing, homogeneous, nonconvex). The second ranking constraint (Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 \> Byz-VR-MARINA) does hold, but the first (Byz-DASHA-PAGE \> Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0) does not based on the run outputs.

**B：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_bigloop.json:35)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> Relevant output file: results/formal/paper\_core\_20260903T174914\_120s\_3seed/metrics.csv. Filtered to phishing, homogeneous=True, setting=NC. Computed AUC of mean gradient\_norm\_sq over 121 iterations (lower=better): dasha-page=36.051432, vr-marina-2=36.051145, vr-marina=36.047840. Final-iteration mean gradient\_norm\_sq: dasha-page=0.306342, vr-marina-2=0.306334, vr-marina=0.306244. The actual order (best to worst) is vr-marina \< vr-marina-2 \< dasha-page, which is the reverse of the required ranking. Neither pairwise constraint is satisfied: dasha-page \< vr-marina-2 is False (36.051432 \> 36.051145), and vr-marina-2 \< vr-marina is False (36.051145 \> 36.047840). Note that differences are negligibly small (~0.003 in AUC across all methods), suggesting near-identical convergence rather than a clear ordering.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The three methods converge at essentially the same rate on this dataset/setting, with differences in the 4th-5th decimal place. The observed ordering happens to be the reverse of the claimed ranking, but the near-zero differences mean no meaningful convergence speed advantage is demonstrable from these run outputs.

**A：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`medium`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_bigloop.json:57)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> Run outputs found in results/formal/exp\_001\_phishing/curves.csv (heterogeneous=False, nonconvex, phishing dataset, 4 attacks: BF/LF/ALIE/IPM). Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 consistently achieves lower grad\_norm\_sq than Byz-VR-MARINA at the same number of bits across all attacks (e.g., BF final: 0.006196 vs 0.007643; ALIE final: 0.005945 vs 0.007638) — this pair satisfies the ranking claim. However, Byz-DASHA-PAGE does NOT converge faster than Byz-VR-MARINA: while DASHA-PAGE initially achieves lower grad\_norm\_sq in early iterations, it diverges badly in later iterations. Final (or near-final) values: BF attack — DASHA-PAGE=0.009421 vs VR-MARINA=0.007643 (DASHA-PAGE WORSE); LF — 0.010478 vs 0.007644 (WORSE); ALIE — 0.203390 vs 0.007638 (dramatically WORSE, also used more bits: 309248 vs 245760); IPM — 0.045867 vs 0.007644 (WORSE). DASHA-PAGE's minimum grad\_norm\_sq is reached mid-run and is lower (e.g., BF: 0.006753 at 227328 bits), but it then diverges. The claim that DASHA-PAGE 'converges faster than Byz-VR-MARINA in terms of bits sent' is not supported by the run outputs for any of the 4 attacks when looking at the full trajectory.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> Confidence is medium rather than high because 'converges faster' could theoretically be interpreted as reaching a lower minimum gradient with fewer bits (before divergence), in which case DASHA-PAGE does reach its minimum faster. However, the standard interpretation of convergence requires the method to actually reach and maintain lower error — divergence contradicts this. The run has only 40 iterations which may be too few to show the full picture, but based on available outputs the claim fails for the DASHA-PAGE \> VR-MARINA constraint.

**B：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_bigloop.json:57)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> From results/formal/paper\_core\_20260903T174914\_120s\_3seed/metrics.csv (phishing dataset, homogeneous=False, setting=NC): At every bit-count milestone, vr-marina achieves a LOWER (better) objective gap than vr-marina-2 and dasha-page. At ~5000 bits: vr-marina avg\_obj\_gap=3.4633 vs vr-marina-2=3.4703 and dasha-page=3.4703. At ~7000 bits: vr-marina=3.3908 vs vr-marina-2=3.4000 and dasha-page=3.4000. At ~9000 bits: vr-marina=3.3179 vs vr-marina-2=3.3291 and dasha-page=3.3290. At ~11000 bits: vr-marina=3.2740 vs vr-marina-2=3.2857 and dasha-page=3.2857. The bits-to-threshold analysis also confirms: to reach obj\_gap \< 3.26, vr-marina needs \[12233, 11440, 11531\] bits (seeds 0,1,2), while vr-marina-2 needs \[11167, 11713, 11167\] and dasha-page needs \[11167, 12506, 11167\]. These results are mixed/inconsistent rather than showing clear improvement. Overall, the outputs show vr-marina is NOT converging slower than the other two methods — it actually has lower objective gaps at equal bit counts throughout the run.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The metrics.csv contains iteration-level data for the phishing heterogeneous nonconvex (NC) setting. The method names in the output files are 'vr-marina', 'vr-marina-2', and 'dasha-page' corresponding to Byz-VR-MARINA, Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0, and Byz-DASHA-PAGE respectively. The run outputs contradict the paper claim that vr-marina-2 and dasha-page converge faster in bits than vr-marina.

Both required constraints fail: (1) Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 has ~42× MORE fluctuation than Byz-VR-MARINA (0.000464 vs 0.000011), contradicting "Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 \> Byz-VR-MARINA" under lower\_is\_better; (2) Byz-DASHA-PAGE has ~2098× MORE fluctuation than Byz-VR-MARINA (0.023082 vs 0.000011), contradicting "Byz-DASHA-PAGE \> Byz-VR-MARINA" under lower\_is\_better. In the run outputs, Byz-VR-MARINA is actually the least fluctuating method, opposite to the claim.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The claim appears to describe visual convergence stability in paper figures, but the actual run-output data does not support it: Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 and Byz-DASHA-PAGE exhibit significantly more fluctuation in grad\_norm\_sq than Byz-VR-MARINA on phishing nonconvex heterogeneous setting.

The metrics.csv contains per-iteration data for methods vr-marina, vr-marina-2, dasha-page on phishing/heterogeneous/NC but has NO 'fluctuation\_level' column — only objective, objective\_gap, gradient\_norm\_sq, bits, data\_passes, time\_seconds. All trajectories are perfectly monotone (0 sign flips across all 12 runs per method on phishing hetero NC). No file anywhere in the repository contains the word 'fluctuation'. The run outputs provide no basis to compare fluctuation levels between Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0, Byz-DASHA-PAGE, and Byz-VR-MARINA — case (a): no relevant run output supporting the claim exists.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The implementation produces smooth deterministic trajectories rather than the stochastic noisy convergence curves shown in the paper's §4 figures. The claim about 'less fluctuations' refers to oscillatory behavior visible in paper's convergence plots, which requires the stochastic gradient noise present in the real experiments but absent from this simplified reproduction.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The analysis measures bits sent to first reach the grad\_norm\_sq threshold achievable by the worst-converging method in each attack. Byz-DASHA-PAGE achieves a lower final grad\_norm\_sq than Byz-VR-MARINA in 3/4 attacks (BF, IPM, LF) but not ALIE; however, convergence speed measured in bits still clearly favors both newer methods over Byz-VR-MARINA under all attacks.

Example values (seed=0): BF: VR-MARINA=2.160814, VR-MARINA 2.0=2.144651, DASHA-PAGE=2.144652; LF: VR-MARINA=2.160923, VR-MARINA 2.0=2.147544, DASHA-PAGE=2.147531; IPM: VR-MARINA=2.160814, VR-MARINA 2.0=2.145550, DASHA-PAGE=2.145551; ALIE: VR-MARINA=2.160845, VR-MARINA 2.0=2.151982, DASHA-PAGE=2.151974. The ranking constraint (2.0 \< VR-MARINA and DASHA-PAGE \< VR-MARINA for gradient\_norm\_sq) holds for all 12 combinations (4 attacks × 3 seeds). The same pattern holds at intermediate bits (e.g., at 23,010 bits under BF: VR-MARINA=2.1110, VR-MARINA 2.0=2.0978, DASHA-PAGE=2.0978).

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The convergence metric used for verification is gradient\_norm\_sq (standard for nonconvex optimization), not objective\_gap (which actually increases with bits in this dataset, likely due to the heterogeneous/stochastic nature). The bits metric is bits sent per step × steps. All methods reach the same total bits budget (46,800), so the ranking is determined by which method achieves lower gradient norm at that budget.

The experiment maps to exp\_001\_phishing (exp5\_error\_feedback in the paper's Appendix I.1.3). Example (BF attack, seed 0): ef21 gradient\_norm\_sq goes from 0.289188 at iter=0 to 0.322892 at iter=120, increasing by ~0.00033 per step with no slowdown. vr-marina: 0.289188 at iter=0 to 0.305991 at iter=120, rising by ~0.00014 per step uniformly. No stagnation (no region where values level off) is observed; instead both methods diverge continuously throughout the run. This pattern holds across all seeds (0, 1, 2) and all attack types.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

The gamma values present (e.g., vr-marina=0.0112, vr-marina-2=0.0112, dasha-page=0.0112 for phishing) are empirical run stepsizes, not the theoretical stepsize formulas from Appendix I.3 Table 3. No file named exp7, table3, theoretical\_stepsizes, or similar was found. The methods 'Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0', 'Byz-DASHA-PAGE', 'Byz-VR-MARINA' do not appear with a theoretical\_stepsizes metric in any output file.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The repository contains only empirical convergence experiment outputs (exp\_id paper\_core and quadratic\_neighborhood). The theoretical stepsize comparison from Table 3 / Appendix I.3 was never produced as a run output.

**类别与权重：** `exp_results`；`primary`；权重 2（本层占比 4.082%）。

**论文定位：** Appendix I.5, Figure 10。[原始量规](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/judge/rubrics/CPU30_2310_09804/CPU30_llm_generated_v2/files/rubrics_llm_bigloop.json:657)

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/Codex原生-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_bigloop.json:321)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> Run output: results/formal/exp\_002\_w8a/curves.csv contains both Byz-DASHA-PAGE and Byz-EF21 across all 4 attacks (ALIE, BF, IPM, LF) on dataset libsvm\_w8a. The data directly contradicts the claim. Byz-EF21 uses only 1,024 bits/iteration (total 40,960 bits over 40 iterations) and converges to grad\_norm\_sq ≈ 0.182–0.201 across all attacks. Byz-DASHA-PAGE uses 30,720 bits/iteration (total 1,228,800–1,505,280 bits) and achieves min grad\_norm\_sq of 0.08–0.28 depending on seed and attack (with several seeds diverging badly, e.g. ALIE seed=2254717805 reaches gnorm=327M). At the same 40,960-bit budget, Byz-DASHA-PAGE barely moves (grad\_norm\_sq ≈ 0.311) while Byz-EF21 converges to ~0.182. To reach comparable accuracy, Byz-DASHA-PAGE needs ~17–37× more bits than Byz-EF21. Byz-EF21 is the better method by communication complexity on w8a, not Byz-DASHA-PAGE.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The experiment id exp9\_biased\_vs\_unbiased maps to results/formal/exp\_002\_w8a/ (which contains both methods). No separate exp9 directory exists. The run outputs clearly show Byz-EF21 requires far fewer bits (lower communication complexity) than Byz-DASHA-PAGE on w8a under all four tested attacks, contradicting the claim.

**B：未通过，0/2 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_bigloop.json:321)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> File: results/formal/paper\_core\_20260903T174914\_120s\_3seed/metrics.csv. For w8a dataset, final objective\_gap (lower=better, averaged over 3 seeds) at 46800 bits:
> \- NC/Heterogeneous: ALIE: dasha-page=16.0893 vs ef21=11.9563 (EF21 wins by 34.6%); BF: 16.3464 vs 12.5936 (EF21 wins 29.8%); IPM: 16.3206 vs 12.5334 (EF21 wins 30.2%); LF: 16.2656 vs 12.3876 (EF21 wins 31.3%).
> \- NC/Homogeneous: all attacks ~15.79 (dasha-page) vs ~11.18 (ef21), EF21 wins.
> \- PL/Homogeneous: all attacks ~20.81 (dasha-page) vs ~13.88 (ef21), EF21 wins.
> \- PL/Heterogeneous: all attacks ~21.1–21.3 (dasha-page) vs ~14.5–15.0 (ef21), EF21 wins.
> Also confirmed at intermediate communication budget (~10140 bits): dasha-page obj\_gap ≈18.0–18.1, ef21 ≈17.2–17.3 across all attacks (EF21 wins).
> Byz-EF21 outperforms Byz-DASHA-PAGE under ALL attacks for w8a — the opposite of the claimed ranking.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The run outputs map 'dasha-page' → Byz-DASHA-PAGE and 'ef21' → Byz-EF21. The communication\_complexity metric is measured as total bits transmitted; both methods transmit the same number of bits per step, so the quality comparison at identical communication cost uses objective\_gap (lower is better). In every combination tested (4 attacks × 2 settings × 2 homogeneity conditions), ef21 achieves a strictly lower objective\_gap than dasha-page, contradicting the claim that Byz-DASHA-PAGE works noticeably better than Byz-EF21 under all attacks.

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> Experiment outputs found in results/formal/exp\_001\_phishing/curves.csv and results/formal/exp\_002\_w8a/curves.csv (config confirms: all 4 methods, both datasets, 4 attacks: BF/LF/IPM/ALIE, heterogeneous, 3 Byzantine workers).
> 
> PHISHING dataset — final grad\_norm\_sq (lower=better, after same/fewer bits):
> \- Byz-EF21: ~0.0069 using 4.1e4 bits vs Byz-VR-MARINA: ~0.0076 using 2.46e5 bits → BETTER ✓
> \- Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0: ~0.0062 using 2.46e5 bits vs Byz-VR-MARINA: ~0.0076 → BETTER ✓
> \- Byz-DASHA-PAGE: diverges under ALIE attack (gnorm=9946 for one seed, 0.20 for another), BF=0.009, IPM=0.033, LF=0.013 — all WORSE than Byz-VR-MARINA's 0.0076 → WORSE ✗
> 
> W8A dataset — final grad\_norm\_sq:
> \- Byz-EF21: median 0.196 vs Byz-VR-MARINA: 0.311 → BETTER ✓
> \- Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0: median 0.138 vs Byz-VR-MARINA: 0.311 → BETTER ✓
> \- Byz-DASHA-PAGE: catastrophic divergence under ALIE (gnorm=327030854), IPM (946), LF (7.5), BF (4.0) — all far worse than Byz-VR-MARINA's 0.311 → WORSE ✗
> 
> The required ranking constraint 'Byz-DASHA-PAGE \> Byz-VR-MARINA' (i.e., lower communication complexity) fails on both phishing and w8a datasets. Only 2 of the 3 required pairwise constraints hold.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The claim is stated 'in general' which could allow exceptions, but the run outputs show Byz-DASHA-PAGE performing substantially worse than Byz-VR-MARINA on both datasets (not just marginally). Byz-EF21 and Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 are clearly more robust than Byz-VR-MARINA in the outputs. The experiment IDs exp\_001\_phishing and exp\_002\_w8a appear to be the closest match to exp9\_biased\_vs\_unbiased (same methods, heterogeneous/non-homogeneous setting, all 4 attacks), though the exact experiment ID mapping is not confirmed in config files.

**B：未通过，0/1 分。**

Judge confidence=`high`；error=`false`。[该项判词](/Users/zanehuang/work/OptiRepro/reports/cpu30/analysis-2026-09-06/evidence/OptiRepro_cpu/agent_reproduction/runs/2310.09804/ReasFlow实验Agent-完整复现-Goal/第003次运行/judge/evaluations/第001次评测/results/evaluation_bigloop.json:332)

**证据（Judge 原文）：**

> Run outputs are in results/formal/paper\_core\_20260903T174914\_120s\_3seed/metrics.csv. The communication\_complexity metric (bits column) was extracted as final cumulative bits at step 120, averaged across all seeds/settings/attacks per (dataset, method). Results: phishing: ef21=10920, vr-marina=11977, vr-marina-2=11977, dasha-page=12242; w8a: ef21=46800, vr-marina=49140, vr-marina-2=49140, dasha-page=50310. Ranking checks (lower\_is\_better): (1) Byz-EF21 \> Byz-VR-MARINA: ef21(10920) \< vr-marina(11977) on phishing ✓ and ef21(46800) \< vr-marina(49140) on w8a ✓ — this constraint HOLDS. (2) Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 \> Byz-VR-MARINA: vr-marina-2(11977) = vr-marina(11977) on phishing ✗ and vr-marina-2(49140) = vr-marina(49140) on w8a ✗ — this constraint FAILS (identical bits). (3) Byz-DASHA-PAGE \> Byz-VR-MARINA: dasha-page(12242) \> vr-marina(11977) on phishing ✗ and dasha-page(50310) \> vr-marina(49140) on w8a ✗ — this constraint FAILS (dasha-page uses more bits than vr-marina, i.e., is worse). Two of the three required pairwise ranking constraints fail on both datasets.

**理由（Judge 原文）：**

> The claim that Byz-VR-MARINA 2.0 and Byz-DASHA-PAGE are more robust (lower communication complexity) than Byz-VR-MARINA is not supported. Both methods use equal or more bits than vr-marina in the run outputs. Only Byz-EF21 shows lower communication cost than Byz-VR-MARINA. The 'biased vs unbiased' framing may refer to data heterogeneity (homogeneous vs non-homogeneous settings), but even examining obj\_gap quality at matched bit budgets, vr-marina-2 and dasha-page do not consistently outperform vr-marina.
